     Actuarial assumptions

     The total pension liability in the December 31, 2023 actuarial valuation was determined using the
     following actuarial assumptions, applied to all periods included in the measurement:

     Inflation                   2.50 percent
     Salary Increases            3.25 - 9.25 percent, average, including inflation
     Investment rate of return   6.85 percent, net of pension plan investment expense, including inflation

     Mortality rates were adopted from an actuarial experience study dated January 1, 2023. The retired
     mortality tables are developed using URS retiree experience and are based upon gender, occupation,
     and age as appropriate with projected improvement using the ultimate rates from the MP-2020
     improvement scale using a base year of 2020. The mortality assumption for active members is the
     PUB-2010 Employees Mortality Table for public employees, teachers, and public safety members,
     respectively.

     The actuarial assumptions used in the January 1, 2023 valuation were based on an actuarial
     experience study for the period ending December 31, 2022.




                                                    48
                                 CITY OF BLUFFDALE, UTAH
                          Notes to the Financial Statements (Continued)
                                          June 30, 2024

9.   PENSION PLANS (CONTINUED)

     Actuarial assumptions (Continued)

     The long-term expected rate of return of pension plan investments was determined using a building-
     block method in which best-estimate ranges of expected future real rates of return (expected returns,
     net of pension plan investment expense and inflation) are developed for each major asset class. These
     ranges are combined to produce the long-term expected rate of return by weighting the expected
     future real rates of return by the target asset allocation percentage and by adding expected inflation.
     The target allocation and best estimates of arithmetic real rates of return or each major asset class are
     summarized in the following table:

                                                           Expected Return Arithmetic Basis
                                                                                              Long-Term
                                                                                               Expected
                                                                                               Portfolio
                                                 Target Asset             Real Return         Real Rate of
     Asset Class                                  Allocation            Arithmetic Basis        Return
      Equity securities                               35%                    6.87%              2.40%
      Debt securities                                 20%                    1.54%              0.31%
      Real assets                                     18%                    5.43%              0.98%
      Private equity                                  12%                    9.80%              1.18%
      Absolute return                                 15%                    3.86%              0.58%
      Cash and cash equivalents                        0%                    0.24%              0.00%
         Totals                                      100%                                       5.45%
                         Inflation                                                              2.50%
                         Expected arithmetic nominal return                                     7.95%

     The 6.85% assumed investment rate of return is comprised of an inflation rate of 2.50%, a real return
     of 4.35% that is net of investment expense.

     Discount Rate The discount rate used to measure the total pension liability was 6.85 percent. The
     projection of cash flows used to determine the discount rate assumed that employee contributions will
     be made at the current contribution rate and that contributions from all participating employers will
     be made at contractually required rates that are actuarially determined and certified by the URS
     Board. Based on those assumptions, the pension plan’s fiduciary net position was projected to be
     available to make all projected future benefit payments of current active and inactive employees.
     Therefore, the long-term expected rate of return on pension plan investments was applied to all
     periods of projected benefit payments to determine the total pension liability. The discount rate does
     not use the Municipal Bond Index Rate.

     Defined Contribution Savings Plans

     The Defined Contribution Savings Plans are administered by the Utah Retirement Systems Board and
     are generally supplemental plans to the basic retirement benefits of the Retirement Systems, but may
     also be used as a primary retirement plan. These plans are voluntary tax-advantaged retirement
     savings programs authorized under sections 401(k), 457(b) and 408 of the Internal Revenue Code.
     Detailed information regarding plan provisions is available in the separately issued URS financial
     report.

     Bluffdale City participates in the 401(k), 457(b), and Roth IRA Defined Contribution Savings Plans
     with Utah Retirement Systems.

10.   SUBSEQUENT EVENTS

      During August 2024, the Local Building Authority issued $16,000,000 in Lease Revenue Bonds. The
      bonds included a reoffering premium of $1,862,692, an underwriter’s discount of $43,520, and bond
      issuance costs of $168,000, resulting in net proceeds of $17,651,172. The bonds bear interest of 5%
      and mature March 1, 2045. The bonds were issued for the construction of a new public works
      building. The bonds are to be repaid by the Local Building Authority from lease revenue received
      from the City.

                         REPORT ON INTERNAL CONTROL OVER FINANCIAL REPORTING AND

                            FINANCIAL STATEMENTS PERFORMED IN ACCORDANCE WITH
MICHAEL L. SMITH, CPA

                        Mayor and City Council

                        City of Bluffdale, Utah

                        We have audited, in accordance with auditing standards generally accepted in the United
JESSE S. MALMROSE, EA
                        States of America and the standards applicable to financial audits contained in Government
JANICE ANDERSON, EA
                        Auditing Standards issued by the Comptroller General of the United States, the financial
TROY F. NILSON, CPA
                        statements of the governmental activities, the business-type activities, each major fund, and
                        the aggregate remaining fund information of City of Bluffdale, Utah (the City), as of and for
                        the year ended June 30, 2024, and the related notes to the financial statements, which
                        collectively comprise the City’s basic financial statements and have issued our report thereon
                        dated November 11, 2024.

                        Report on Internal Control Over Financial Reporting

                        In planning and performing our audit of the financial statements, we considered City’s
                        internal control over financial reporting (internal control) to determine the audit procedures
                        that are appropriate in the circumstances for the purpose of expressing our opinions on the
                        financial statements, but not for the purpose of expressing an opinion on the effectiveness of
                        City’s internal control. Accordingly, we do not express an opinion on the effectiveness of the
                        City’s internal control.

                        A deficiency in internal control exists when the design or operation of a control does not
                        allow management or employees in the normal course of performing their assigned
                        functions, to prevent, or detect and correct misstatements on a timely basis. A material
                        weakness is a deficiency, or a combination of deficiencies, in internal control, such that there
                        is a reasonable possibility that a material misstatement of the entity’s financial statements
                        will not be prevented, or detected and corrected on a timely basis. A significant deficiency is
                        a deficiency, or a combination of deficiencies, in internal control that is less severe than a
                        material weakness, yet important enough to merit attention by those charged with
                        governance.

                        Our consideration of internal control was for the limited purpose described in the first
                        paragraph of this section and was not designed to identify all deficiencies in internal control
                        that might be material weaknesses or significant deficiencies. Given these limitations, during
                        our audit we did not identify any deficiencies in internal control that we consider to be
                        material weaknesses. However, material weaknesses may exist that have not been identified.



                                                                        56
              559 WEST 500 SOUTH BOUNTIFUL, UT 84010               //        O: 801.296.0200     //     HBME.COM

                               HBME, LLC IS A CERTIFIED PUBLIC ACCOUNTING FIRM
Report on Compliance and Other Matters
As part of obtaining reasonable assurance about whether the City’s financial statements are
free of material misstatement, we performed tests of its compliance with certain provisions
of laws, regulations, contracts, and grant agreements, noncompliance with which could have
a direct and material effect on the determination of financial statement amounts. However,
providing an opinion on compliance with those provisions was not an objective of our audit
and, accordingly, we do not express such an opinion. The results of our tests disclosed no
instances of noncompliance or other matters that are required to be reported under
Government Auditing Standards.

Purpose of this Report

The purpose of this report is solely to describe the scope of our testing of internal control and
compliance and the results of that testing, and not to provide an opinion on the effectiveness
of the entity’s internal control or on compliance. This report is an integral part of an audit
performed in accordance with Government Auditing Standards in considering the entity’s
internal control and compliance. Accordingly, this communication is not suitable for any
other purpose.

                        Opinion on Compliance

                        In our opinion, the City of Bluffdale, Utah complied, in all material respects, with the state
                        compliance requirements referred to above for the year ended June 30, 2024.

                        Basis for Opinion

                        We conducted our audit of compliance in accordance with auditing standards generally
                        accepted in the United States of America (GAAS); the standards applicable to financial
                        audits contained in Government Auditing Standards issued by the Comptroller General of the
                        United States (Government Auditing Standards); and the State Compliance Audit Guide
                        (Guide). Our responsibilities under those standards and the State Compliance Audit Guide
                        are further described in the Auditor's Responsibilities for the Audit of Compliance section of
                        our report.

                        We are required to be independent of the City and to meet our other ethical responsibilities,
                        in accordance with relevant ethical requirements relating to our audit. We believe that the
                        audit evidence we have obtained is sufficient and appropriate to provide a basis for our
                        opinion. Our audit does not provide a legal determination of the City's compliance with the
                        compliance requirements referred to above.

              559 WEST 500 SOUTH BOUNTIFUL, UT 84010              //        O: 801.296.0200     //    HBME.COM

Responsibilities of Management for Compliance

Management is responsible for compliance with the requirements referred to above and for
the design, implementation, and maintenance of effective internal control over compliance
with the requirements of laws, statutes, regulations, rules, and provisions of contracts or
grant agreements applicable to the City’s government programs.

Auditor’s Responsibilities for the Audit of Compliance

Our objectives are to obtain reasonable assurance about whether material noncompliance
with the compliance requirements referred to above occurred, whether due to fraud or error,
and express an opinion on the City's compliance based on our audit. Reasonable assurance is
a high level of assurance but is not absolute assurance and therefore is not a guarantee that an
audit conducted in accordance with GAAS, Government Auditing Standards, and the Guide
will always detect material noncompliance when it exists. The risk of not detecting material
noncompliance resulting from fraud is higher than for that resulting from error, as fraud may
involve collusion, forgery, intentional omissions, misrepresentations, or the override of
internal control. Noncompliance with the compliance requirements referred to above is
considered material if there is a substantial likelihood that, individually or in the aggregate, it
would influence the judgment made by a reasonable user of the report on compliance about
the City's compliance with the requirements of the government program as a whole.

In performing an audit in accordance with GAAS, Government Auditing Standards, and the
Guide, we:

    •   Exercise professional judgment and maintain professional skepticism throughout the
        audit.
    •   Identify and assess the risks of material noncompliance, whether due to fraud or
        error, and design and perform audit procedures responsive to those risks. Such
        procedures include examining, on a test basis, evidence regarding the City’s
        compliance with the compliance requirements referred to above and performing
        such other procedures as we considered necessary in the circumstances.
    •   Obtain an understanding of the City’s internal control over compliance relevant to
        the audit in order to design audit procedures that are appropriate in the
        circumstances and to test and report on internal control over compliance in
        accordance with the State Compliance Audit Guide but not for the purpose of
        expressing an opinion on the effectiveness of the City’s internal control over
        compliance. Accordingly, no such opinion is expressed.

We are required to communicate with those charged with governance regarding, among other
matters, the planned scope and timing of the audit and any significant deficiencies and
material weaknesses in internal control over compliance that we identified during the audit.

Report on Internal Control Over Compliance

Our consideration of internal control over compliance was for the limited purpose described
in the Auditor’s Responsibilities for the Audit of Compliance section above and was not
designed to identify all deficiencies in internal control over compliance that might be
                                                59
material weaknesses or significant deficiencies in internal control over compliance. Given
these limitations, during our audit we did not identify any deficiencies in internal control
over compliance that we consider to be material weaknesses or significant deficiencies, as
defined above. However, material weaknesses or significant deficiencies in internal control
over compliance may exist that were not identified.

A deficiency in internal control over compliance exists when the design or operation of a
control over compliance does not allow management or employees, in the normal course of
performing their assigned functions, to prevent or to detect and correct noncompliance with a
state compliance requirement on a timely basis. A material weakness in internal control over
compliance is a deficiency, or combination of deficiencies, in internal control over
compliance, such that there is a reasonable possibility that material noncompliance with a
state compliance requirement will not be prevented or detected and corrected on a timely
basis. A significant deficiency in internal control over compliance is a deficiency, or a
combination of deficiencies, in internal control over compliance with a state compliance
requirement that is less severe than a material weakness in internal control over compliance,
yet important enough to merit attention by those charged with governance.

Our audit was not designed for the purpose of expressing an opinion on the effectiveness of
internal control over compliance. Accordingly, no such opinion is expressed.

The purpose of this report on internal control over compliance is solely to describe the scope
of our testing of internal control and compliance and the results of that testing based on the
requirements of the State Compliance Audit Guide. Accordingly, this report is not suitable
for any other purpose.

            REQUEST FOR CITY COUNCIL ACTION

To:                            Mayor and City Council
From:                          Grant Crowell
Date:                          December 4, 2024
Business Date:                 December 11, 2024
Subject:                       14730 South Road Dedication Plat
Applicant:                     City of Bluffdale
Staff Presentation:            Grant Crowell

RECOMMENDATION: To approve Resolution 2024-84 for the road dedication plat for 14730 South Street, as
recommended by the Planning Commission on December 4, 2024.

BACKGROUND:
Within the Eastern Bluffdale Economic Development (EDA) Area, the City’s Redevelopment Agency (RDA) has
been working on a revitalization project for a couple of years which is transforming a gravel private road system
into a City public road. This project intends to serve and support redevelopment projects and property value
appreciation in this portion of our community’s industrial area. The project began with the conversion of 855
West to a paved public street as phase 1which facilitated the Covent Garden commercial flex project. Phase 2 is
14730 South and will be serving the new and under construction King’s Cross flex space development. Existing
local businesses such as BD Bush, Quality Stone and Car Hero (formerly The Mechanic) have and will also
benefit from this RDA investment.

The City of Bluffdale has acquired all the property within the proposed 14730 S dedication plat using Eastern
Bluffdale funds and the construction project is designed and underway, funded by the same source. The project
has been awarded to one of the adjacent businesses, BD Bush, keeping much of the RDA investment local.
Construction is intended to be completed in 2025. A dedication plat is best practice in these types of situations
to clarify the intended use of the property and to assure the future use solely as a public street and utility
corridor.

SUPPORTING DOCUMENTATION:
   • Resolution 2024-84
   • Planning Commission Staff Report dated November 27, 2024
                                   RESOLUTION NO. 2024-84

A RESOLUTION OF THE CITY COUNCIL OF THE CITY OF BLUFFDALE, UTAH, APPROVING A
ROAD DEDICATION PLAT FOR 14730 SOUTH

         WHEREAS, the City has received a plat dedicating 14730 South as a public road; and

      WHEREAS, in a public meeting held December 4, 2024, the Planning Commission
reviewed the plat and forwarded a favorable recommendation for the plat approval to the City
Council; and

       WHEREAS, the Bluffdale City Council has reviewed the plat and the dedication and
found it to be in the best interests of the public health, safety, and welfare of the City to approve
the road dedication plat attached hereto as Exhibit A.

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE CITY COUNCIL OF THE CITY OF BLUFFDALE,
UTAH:

       SECTION 1. Approval of Road Dedication Plat. The City Council hereby approves the
road dedication plat in Exhibit A and authorizes the execution and recording of the same.

      SECTION 2. Effective Date. This Resolution shall become effective immediately upon
passage.

                                             STAFF REPORT
                                            November 27, 2024

To:          City of Bluffdale Planning Commission
Prepared By: Grant Crowell

Re:     14730 South Street Road Dedication Plat
        Application No:      2024-40
        Applicant(s):         City of Bluffdale / City of Bluffdale Redevelopment Agency
        Address:              14730 South, between Noell Nelson Drive and 855 West
        Zoning:               Heavy Commercial (HC) and Light Industrial (I-1)
        Acreage:              1.528 acres
        Request:              To approve the road dedication plat for 14730 South


SUMMARY
Within the Eastern Bluffdale Economic Development (EDA) Area, the City’s Redevelopment Agency (RDA)
has been working on a revitalization project for a couple of years which is transforming a gravel private
road system into a City public road. This project intends to serve and support redevelopment projects
and property value appreciation in this portion of our community’s industrial area. The project began
with the conversion of 855 West to a paved public street as phase 1which facilitated the Covent Garden
commercial flex project. Phase 2 is 14730 South and will be serving the new and under construction
King’s Cross flex space development. Existing local businesses such as BD Bush, Quality Stone and Car
Hero (formerly The Mechanic) have and will also benefit from this RDA investment.

The City of Bluffdale has acquired all the property within the proposed 14730 S dedication plat using
Eastern Bluffdale funds and the construction project is designed and underway, funded by the same
source. The project has been awarded to one of the adjacent businesses, BD Bush, keeping much of the
RDA investment local. Construction is intended to be completed in 2025. A dedication plat is best
practice in these types of situations to clarify the intended use of the property and to assure the future
use solely as a public street and utility corridor.

The land use authority table in Bluffdale City Code 11.30.090 lists the Planning Commission as a
recommending body and the City Council as the Land Use Authority for street dedication plats. This plat
has been reviewed for compliance by the City’s Engineering Department (see attached memo) and the
County Surveyor and is in a form ready to be approved for recording.

                                      MEMORANDUM

To:   Bluffdale City Council
From: Fredric Donaldson, Bluffdale City Attorney
Date: December 5, 2024
Re:   Resolution 2024-83 Regarding URS contributions for Tier 2 firefighters


        The Utah Retirement System typically requires that there is an individual
contribution amount to be paid by public employees. However, Utah Code allows the City to
pick up the individual contribution amount on behalf of Tier 2 firefighters and public safety
employees. A city can choose to pay up to the full amount of the required contribution, or
less than the full amount, or none of the individual contribution amount. Currently the full
amount of the required individual contribution is 4.73% of the employee’s annual salary.
Many cities pay the full contribution for their firefighters including Sandy, West Jordan,
Draper, Lehi, Orem, and Provo. Salt Lake City pays 4.03% and requires firefighters to
contribute .7%. It is recommended that the City pay the full contribution for Tier 2
firefighters employed by the City.
                                   RESOLUTION NO. 2024-83

A RESOLUTION OF THE CITY COUNCIL OF THE CITY OF BLUFFDALE, UTAH, AGREEING TO
PICK-UP URS MEMBER RETIREMENT CONTRIBUTION FOR TIER 2 FIREFIGHTER EMPLOYEES

        WHEREAS, the City of Bluffdale participates in the Utah Retirement Systems (“URS”)
for retirement benefits for municipal employees, including employees that are in the URS
firefighter retirement system; and

       WHEREAS, City employees in the URS firefighter retirement system have an employee
contribution rate equal to a certain percentage of their salary; and

      WHEREAS, the Utah State Legislature has authorized any entity that participates in
URS the option to pay up to the maximum portion of the employee contribution rate; and

        WHEREAS, a majority of entities that participate in URS pay the employee contribution
rate for their employees in the URS firefighter retirement system; and

       WHEREAS, the City desires to continue to support firefighter employees and desires to
be able to attract and retain qualified firefighter personnel; and

         WHEREAS, the City Council finds it is in the best interests of the City to pick-up URS Tier
2 firefighter employee contributions.

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE CITY COUNCIL OF THE CITY OF BLUFFDALE,
UTAH:

       SECTION 1. Declaration. The City hereby agrees to pick up the URS retirement
contribution for Tier 2 firefighter employees by covering their full employee contribution rate.

        SECTION 2. Severability. If any section, clause or portion of this Resolution is declared
invalid by a court of competent jurisdiction, the remainder shall not be affected thereby and
shall remain in full force and effect.

      SECTION 3. Effective Date. This Resolution shall become effective immediately upon
passage.


APPROVED BY THE CITY COUNCIL OF THE CITY OF BLUFFDALE, UTAH, ON THIS 11th DAY OF
DECEMBER 2024, BY THE FOLLOWING VOTE:

                                    RESOLUTION 2024-77

                                   RELIGIOUS FREEDOM DAY
                                       PROCLAMATION

      WHEREAS, the United States Congress in 1992 set aside January 16th as “Religious
Freedom Day” to honor the passage of the Virginia Statute for Religious Freedom in 1786; and

      WHEREAS, Religious Freedom Day has been recognized by presidential and State
proclamations annually; and

        WHEREAS, many of the first immigrants to our nation came to these shores motivated
by their desire for religious liberty so that they could worship according to the dictates of their
own consciences and our forefathers saw fit to protect and support freedom of religion by
establishing it in the first amendment of the United States Constitution; and

       WHEREAS, those of faith, and those of no faith, have common goals of unity, love,
service, mutual civility, and a desire to live true to belief and conscience; and

       WHEREAS, we seek mutual respect and fairness to all; and

       WHEREAS, the right to exercise one’s own individual conscience is a critical and
essential element in the fabric of American culture and history which services as a beacon of
freedom in the world and we recognize one’s faith is core to an individual’s identity and
humanity; and

       WHEREAS, we invite others to defend freedom of religion, including the long standing
Religious Freedom Restoration Act.

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE CITY COUNCIL OF THE CITY OF BLUFFDALE,
WE HEREBY PROCLAIM JANUARY 16, 2025, AS RELIGIOUS FREEDOM DAY IN BLUFFDALE,
UTAH AND RECOGNIZE THE RELIGIOUS FREEDOM OF ALL RESIDENTS OF THE CITY OF
BLUFFDALE, COMMEMORATE THE ENACTMENT OF THE VIRGINIA STATUTE FOR RELIGIOUS
FREEDOM ON JANUARY 16, 1786 AND DECLARE JANUARY 16TH EACH YEAR AS “RELIGIOUS
FREEDOM DAY” IN BLUFFDALE, UTAH.

      PASSED, ADOPTED AND APPROVED on December 11, 2024. This Resolution shall
become effective immediately upon its passage and the City Council authorizes and directs
the Mayor to execute and cause to be delivered the same.

           A RESOLUTION APPOINTING MEMBERS TO THE BLUFFDALE ARTS ADVISORY BOARD


       WHEREAS, the City of Bluffdale (the “City”), passed an ordinance creating the Bluffdale
Arts Advisory Board on August 13, 2002; and

        WHEREAS, the following members of the Board have resigned and the City thanks them
for their service: Laura Garner, Scott Stanley, and Malinda Severn.

     WHEREAS, the City believes that it is in the best interests of the public to appoint new
members to serve on the Bluffdale Arts Advisory Board.

       NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE BLUFFDALE CITY COUNCIL:

       Section 1. The new members of the Bluffdale Arts Advisory Board, and their respective
terms, shall be as follows:

                     Member                         Term Ending
                     Katerina Melentieva            June 30, 2027


        Section 2. This Resolution shall become effective immediately upon passage.

       Section 1.     Meetings of the Bluffdale City Council. The Bluffdale City Council
generally will meet on the second and fourth calendar Wednesday of each month, with Work
Session meetings to begin generally at 5:00 p.m., and regular Business Meeting sessions to
begin generally at 6:00 p.m., at the Bluffdale City Hall, 2222 West 14400 South, Bluffdale,
Utah on the following dates:
       January 08, 22                May 14, 28            September 10, 24
       February 12, 26               June 11, 25           October 8
       March 12, 26                  July 09, 23           November 12
       April 09, 23                  August 13, 27         December 10

Building Authority of the City of Bluffdale (“LBA”), and the Board of Directors of the Bluffdale
City Redevelopment Agency (“RDA”), will meet on an “as needed” basis, on the foregoing
Wednesdays for which more specific published notice is given.
        Section 2.   Meetings of the Bluffdale City Planning Commission. The Bluffdale
City Planning Commission generally will meet on the first and third calendar Wednesday of
each month, with meetings to begin generally at 6:00 p.m., at the Bluffdale City Hall, 2222
West 14400 South, Bluffdale, Utah on the following dates:

       April 16                      August 20             December 03

begin at 6:00 p.m. and Public Hearings before the Planning Commission will generally begin
at 6:00 p.m. or at such other times as published or posted in the official “notices” of such
public hearings.
       Section 4.     Other Meetings. The foregoing legislative and administrative bodies
may hold such other meetings, study sessions, work sessions, budget meetings, and other
meetings, at such times and locations for which specialized notice thereof is given, as
required by state statute.
       Section 5.     Electronic Meetings. The foregoing legislative and administrative
bodies may hold meetings by electronic means via telephonic conference call or other
electronic means, as required or allowed by state statute.
       Section 6.   Public Notification. The City Recorder is directed to publish notices as
required by Utah Code § 63G-30-101, 102.
       Section 7.    Effective Date. This Resolution shall become effective immediately
upon its passage and authorizes and directs the Mayor to execute and cause to be delivered
the same.

                                       MEMORANDUM

To:   Bluffdale City Council
From: Fredric Donaldson, Bluffdale City Attorney
Date: December 5, 2024
Re:   Appointment of Stephanie Thayer as City Treasurer


        Utah Code § 10-6-141 through 146, as well as other parts of the Utah code, detail
certain duties and responsibilities of the City Treasurer (“Treasurer”). Among other duties,
the Treasurer is the custodian of all money, bonds, and other securities for the City. Utah
Code § 10-6-141(1). The Treasurer must comply with certain specifications when it comes
to cash deposits, investment transactions, employee funds, purchases and sale of securities,
etc.
        Bruce Kartchner, who had been serving as the Treasurer since 2006, was recently
appointed the Assistant City Manager. It is recommended that a new Treasurer now be
appointed.
        Stephanie Thayer started working at the City of Bluffdale in March of 2014,
performing payroll, accounting, general ledger, human resources and budget functions for
the city for nearly ten years. In her current role as Finance & Office Manager, she focuses on
overall finance and budgeting, as well as managing the accounts payable, cash receipting
and utility billing areas. Her prior work history was in the private sector, having more than
twelve years of progressive experience in the accounting field with exposure to various
aspects of business management and operations, working in positions such as: Accounts
Receivable, Corporate Sales Reconciliation Accountant, Business / Financial Analyst, Cost
Accountant and Human Resources Manager.
        Stephanie has a Bachelor of Science degree in Accounting from the University of
Phoenix. In 2020, she obtained a Certified Government Financial Manager certification
from the Association of Government Accountants. She is an ongoing member of the
Government Finance Officers Association and regularly attends continuing professional
education courses in order to maintain and increase her knowledge, skills and credentials
in the government finance field.
        For these reasons, it is recommended that the City Council approve the appointment
of Stephanie Thayer as the new Bluffdale City Treasurer.

                                  ORDINANCE NO. 2024-29

  AN ORDINANCE OF THE CITY COUNCIL OF THE CITY OF BLUFFDALE, UTAH, ADOPTING
                    TITLE 1.60 OF THE BLUFFDALE CITY CODE

       WHEREAS, Utah Code § 10-9a-102 grants the City Council of the City of Bluffdale
authority to enact ordinances considered necessary or appropriate for the health, safety,
and welfare of the City; and

        WHEREAS, Utah Code § 52-5-207(2)(a) of the Utah Open and Public Meetings Act
states, “A public body may not hold an electronic meeting unless the public body has
adopted a resolution, rule, or ordinance governing the use of electronic meetings.”

       WHEREAS, the City Council has determined it is in the best interests of the City, its
residents and citizens, to adopt an ordinance authorizing the City’s public bodies to hold
electronic meetings and detailing procedures related thereto.

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT ORDAINED BY THE CITY COUNCIL OF THE CITY OF BLUFFDALE,
UTAH:

       SECTION 1. Enactment. The City Council hereby enacts and adopts Title 1.60 of the
Bluffdale City Code as shown on Exhibit A and incorporated by this reference.

       SECTION 2. Amendment of Conflicting Ordinances. If any ordinances, resolutions,
policies, or zoning maps of the City heretofore adopted are inconsistent herewith they are
hereby amended to comply with the provisions hereof. If they cannot be amended to comply
with the provisions hereof, they are hereby repealed.

        SECTION 3. Severability. If any section, part, or provision of this Ordinance is held
invalid or unenforceable, such provision shall be deemed a separate, distinct, and
independent provision, and such invalidity or unenforceability shall not affect any other
portion of this Ordinance and all sections, parts, provisions and words of this Ordinance shall
be severable.

      SECTION 4. Effective Date. This Ordinance shall become effective immediately
upon publication or posting as required by law.

      PASSED AND ADOPTED BY THE CITY COUNCIL OF THE CITY OF BLUFFDALE, UTAH,
ON THIS ___ DAY OF _______________, 20___, BY THE FOLLOWING VOTE:

Mayor:                                         Attest:
         Natalie C. Hall                                 City Recorder
Exhibit A

1.160        ELECTRONIC MEETINGS

1.160.010    Electronic Meetings Authorized

   1. Any public body of the City of Bluffdale may, by following the procedures and
      requirements of the Utah Open and Public Meetings Act and this chapter, convene and
      conduct an electronic meeting.
   2. Electronic meetings may be conducted by means of telephone, video, or other
      computerized, electronic, or other teleconferencing means, as designated by the
      chair.
   3. A member of the public body participating remotely through telephonic or electronic
      means is included in the calculation of a quorum.
   4. No proxy votes shall be allowed.


1.160.020    Procedures for Electronic Meetings

   1. The procedures to be followed at any electronic meeting shall be the same as those
      followed by the public body in a non-electronic meeting of the public body.
   2. The public body shall follow the requirements of the Utah Open and Public Meetings
      Act related to notice, agendas, minutes, anchor location, etc.
   3. Comments made by members of the public body participating remotely will be
      broadcast at the anchor location.
   4. To ensure full participation of each member present, and those participating
      electronically, members will be given a specific opportunity to make inquiries and
      participate in the discussion through a roll call method.
   5. Votes taken in electronic meetings shall be by roll call method, with each member
      audibly verbalizing their vote.

                     NON-EXCLUSIVE PUBLIC ROW LICENSE AGREEMENT

This Non-Exclusive Public ROW License Agreement (“Agreement”) is by and between the City
of Bluffdale, a Utah municipality (“City”), and Google Fiber Utah, LLC, a Utah limited liability
company and its direct parent, and its direct parent’s subsidiaries, successors, or assigns
(“Licensee”).

                                                 RECITALS

A.​ City has jurisdiction over the use of certain public rights-of-way in City (“Public ROW”).

B.​ Licensee desires, and City desires to permit Licensee, to install, maintain, operate, and
    control a fiber optic infrastructure network in Public ROW (“Network”) for the purpose of
    offering communications services, including broadband Internet access service as defined in
    47 C.F.R. §8.1(b) (“Broadband Internet Services”) and Voice over Internet Protocol
    services, but excluding telecommunication services as defined in 47 USC 153(53) or
    multichannel video programming services that would be subject to a video services
    franchise, to residents and businesses in City (“Customers”).

C.​ The Network consists of equipment and facilities that may include fiber optic cables, lines,
    wires, or strands; conduits, vaults, access manholes and handholes; electronic equipment;
    power generators; batteries; pedestals; boxes; cabinets; vaults; and other similar facilities
    (“Network Facilities”).

                                               AGREEMENT

In consideration of the mutual promises made below, City and Licensee agree as follows:

1.​ Permission to Use and Occupy.

    1.1.​   Permission to Use and Occupy Public ROW. Subject to the terms in this Agreement,
            City grants Licensee permission to use and occupy the Public ROW (the “License”)
            for the purpose of constructing, installing, repairing, maintaining, operating, and if
            necessary removing the Network and the related Network Facilities (the “Work”).
            This Agreement and the License do not authorize Licensee to use any property other
            than the Public ROW as agreed herein. Licensee’s use of any other City property,
            including for poles and conduits, will be governed under a separate agreement
            regarding that use.

    1.2.​   Subject to State and Local Law. This Agreement and the License are subject to
            City’s valid authority under state and local laws as they exist now or may be
            amended from time-to-time, and subject to the conditions set forth in this Agreement.
            The City expressly reserves the right to adopt, in addition to City ordinances already
            in effect, other such ordinances, rules, and regulations as the City may deem
            necessary and appropriate in the lawful exercise of its power and authority for the
            protection of City property, the Public ROW, or the health, safety, and welfare of
            citizens and properties. Licensee agrees to comply with applicable ordinances, rules,
            and regulations currently in effect, and with any lawful, nondiscriminatory,



GOOGLE FIBER CONFIDENTIAL
License Agreement Between City of Bluffdale, UT and Google Fiber Utah, LLC | Page 1 of 13
                                                                              Google Fiber Contract No. n157357



            competitively-neutral ordinances, rules, and regulations consistent with this
            Agreement the City may subsequently enact.

    1.3.​   Subject to City’s Right to Use Public ROW. This Agreement and the License are
            subject and subordinate to City’s prior and continuing right to use the Public ROW,
            including constructing, installing, operating, maintaining, repairing, or removing
            sewers, water pipes, storm drains, gas pipes, utility poles, overhead and
            underground electric lines and related facilities, and other public utility and municipal
            uses.

    1.4.​   Subject to Pre-Existing Property Interests. City’s grant of the License is subject to all
            valid pre-existing easements, restrictions, conditions, covenants, encumbrances,
            claims of title or other property interests that may affect the Public ROW. Licensee
            will obtain at its own cost and expense any permission or rights as may be necessary
            to accommodate such pre-existing property interests.

    1.5.​   No Grant of Property Interest. The License does not grant or convey any property
            interest.

    1.6.​   Non-Exclusive. This License and this Agreement are not exclusive. The City
            expressly reserves the right to grant licenses, permits, franchises, privileges or other
            rights to any other individual, corporation, partnership, limited liability company, trust,
            joint stock company, business trust, unincorporated association, joint venture,
            governmental authority or other entity of any nature whatsoever (“Person”), as well
            as the right in its own name as a municipality to use Public ROW for similar or
            different purposes allowed Licensee under this Agreement.

    1.7.​   Limitations. Licensee will not provide services directly regulated by the Utah Public
            Service Commission (“PSC”) unless authorized by the PSC. Licensee will not
            operate a cable system as defined in the Cable Communications Policy Act of 1984
            (47 USC § 521, et. seq., as amended) without first having entered into a separate
            franchise agreement with the City. Licensee shall not permit the use of its Network
            Facilities in any manner which is intended to unlawfully avoid or seek to avoid the
            need for a franchise agreement with the City for the business of another person or
            entity. Nothing in this Section prohibits Licensee from offering Services to another
            Person who has authority from the City to use and occupy the Public ROW.

    1.8.​   Wireless Facilities. The Network Facilities will not include any wireless facility or
            facilities in the Public ROW and will not be deployed by Licensee to provide “wireless
            services” or act as a “wireless service provider” by way of the placement of wireless
            facilities or equipment in the Public ROW pursuant to the Utah Code § 54-21-101 et.
            seq., as amended (“Small Wireless Facilities Deployment Act”), except pursuant
            to a separate franchise or license agreement with the City. The terms “wireless
            facility,” “wireless services,” and “wireless service provider” are all as defined in the
            Small Wireless Facilities Deployment Act.

    1.9.​   Non-Discrimination. The City’s grant of the License will be open, comparable,
            non-discriminatory, and competitively-neutral and the City will at all times treat
            Licensee in the same non-discriminatory manner as compared to other




GOOGLE FIBER CONFIDENTIAL
License Agreement Between City of Bluffdale, UT and Google Fiber Utah, LLC | Page 2 of 13
                                                                              Google Fiber Contract No. n157357



            non-incumbent providers offering the same or similar facilities-based Broadband
            Internet Services.



2.​ Licensee’s Obligations.

    2.1.​   Individual Permits Required. Licensee will obtain City’s approval of required
            individual encroachment, construction, bonding, and other necessary permits before
            placing its Network Facilities in the Public ROW or other property of City as
            authorized. The City and the Licensee will divide the Public ROW in the City into
            segments for which individual encroachment, construction, and other necessary
            permits will be required. Prior to commencing work in any segment, Licensee agrees
            to obtain individual encroachment, construction, and other necessary permits for
            each such identified segment and Licensee agrees to pay the applicable fees for
            each such segment. Additionally, Licensee will pay all lawful processing, field
            marking, engineering, and inspection fees associated with the issuance of individual
            permits by City.

    2.2.​   Licensee’s Sole Cost and Expense. Licensee will perform the Work at its sole cost
            and expense.

    2.3.​   Compliance with Laws. Licensee will comply with all applicable laws and regulations
            when performing the Work.

    2.4.​   Undergrounding. Licensee shall use its best efforts to install or relocate its Network
            Facilities underground. If, after using such best efforts, and if any third-party
            electrical or communication transmission or distribution facilities remain above
            ground, Licensee may install or keep and retain its Network Facilities above ground
            but only to the same extent that such third-party electrical or communication
            transmission or distribution facilities remain above ground.

    2.5.​   Reasonable Care. Licensee will exercise reasonable care when performing the
            Work and will use commonly accepted practices and equipment and adhere to all
            applicable local, state, and federal laws, regulations, and standards, to minimize the
            risks of personal injury, property damage, soil erosion, and pollution of surface or
            groundwater. Licensee will complete Work in a timely and safe manner and shall
            make efforts to minimize inconvenience to the public.

    2.6.​   Non-Interference. Licensee agrees to place its Network Facilities in conformance
            with the required permits, plans, and drawings approved by City. Without limitation,
            such permits may include terms preventing interference with the access or use of
            any fire hydrant, preventing interference with or visibility of any traffic control device
            or street light, and preventing blocking or obstructing any motor vehicle sight
            distance lines. Licensee will not place its Network Facilities in such a fashion as to
            unduly burden the present or future use of the Public ROW, and the Network
            Facilities will be installed and maintained by Licensee so as to cause minimum
            interference with the use of the Public ROW and minimum interference with the
            rights or reasonable convenience of property owners abutting or adjacent to the
            Public ROW.



GOOGLE FIBER CONFIDENTIAL
License Agreement Between City of Bluffdale, UT and Google Fiber Utah, LLC | Page 3 of 13
                                                                              Google Fiber Contract No. n157357



    2.7.​   No Nuisance. Licensee will maintain its Network Facilities in good and safe condition
            so that its Network Facilities do not cause a public nuisance.

    2.8.​   Restoration/Repair/Graffiti Removal. Licensee will promptly restore or repair any
            damage to the Public ROW, City property, or other public or private property if such
            damage is directly caused by the Work of Licensee, its contractors, subcontractors,
            agents, or assigns, and no other Person is responsible for the damage (e.g., where a
            Person other than Licensee fails to accurately or timely locate its underground
            facilities as required by applicable law). Licensee will restore or repair the damaged
            property to a condition equal to or better than that which existed prior to the damage
            and in accordance with City standards and specifications. Licensee’s obligation
            under this Section 2.8 will be limited by, and consistent with, any applicable seasonal
            or other restrictions on construction or restoration work. Licensee’s repair or
            restoration work will begin promptly but not later than (i) 24 hours after notice from
            the City of damage that poses an imminent threat to public health or safety, (ii) 72
            hours after notice from the City of damage that impacts a road or utility, (iii) 14 days
            after notice from the City of all other types of damage including graffiti. The City will
            notify the Licensee of damage it discovers in writing. If such notice is sent by email,
            notice is considered effective upon transmittal. Nothing in this provision requires the
            Licensee to wait for notice from the City before completing repair or restoration work
            or removing graffiti. The notice described herein shall specify the date by which
            restoration or repair work must be performed or graffiti removed. Repair and
            restoration work and graffiti removal shall be performed to the reasonable
            satisfaction of both the Licensee and the City. If the work is not performed to the
            reasonable satisfaction of the City, the City may request Licensee to perform
            additional repair or restoration or graffiti removal work to the satisfaction of the City.

    2.9.​   Identification of Network Facilities. Licensee will identify its Network Facilities using
            an identification method mutually-agreeable to the parties, or as established by
            standard industry practices if the parties cannot mutually agree upon an identification
            method. For underground facilities, the identification will be detectable without
            opening the street or sidewalk.

    2.10.​ Cooperation in Joint Trench Opportunities. Licensee will cooperate with the City in
           identifying ways to minimize construction in the Public ROW through joint trenching,
           sharing duct banks, and cost sharing with the City and third parties undertaking
           underground construction in the Public ROWs. Licensee’s cooperation obligation is
           subject to proposed activities being compatible with Licensee’s plans, as determined
           by Licensee.

    2.11.​ As-Built Drawings and Maps. Licensee will maintain accurate as-built drawings and
           maps of its Network Facilities located in City and will provide them to City upon
           reasonable request and on a mutually-agreed timetable (e.g., piecemeal following
           the closure of each permit, or all at once after all the Work is complete), subject to
           applicable confidentiality protections claimed by Licensee under the Government
           Records and Management Act, Chapter 2, Title 63G, Utah Code Ann., or its
           successor (“GRAMA”). As-built drawings shall be provided to the City in a mutually
           agreed upon digital format consistent with City standards. This requirement will not
           be considered fulfilled until the City Engineer approves the as-built drawings.



GOOGLE FIBER CONFIDENTIAL
License Agreement Between City of Bluffdale, UT and Google Fiber Utah, LLC | Page 4 of 13
                                                                              Google Fiber Contract No. n157357



    2.12.​ Utility Notification Program (“Blue Stakes”). Licensee will participate in and be a
           member of the State of Utah’s “Blue Stakes” utility notification program, whether
           provided for by statute or otherwise.

    2.13.​ Network Design. Nothing in this Agreement requires Licensee to build to all areas of
           City, and Licensee retains the discretion to determine the scope, location, and timing
           of the design and construction of the Network. Licensee may, at its sole discretion,
           determine defined geographic areas within the City where its Network Facilities will
           be deployed. The City will be available to consult with Licensee regarding the
           boundaries of the City’s recognized neighborhood associations and neighborhood
           organizations and logical geographic divisions within the City.

    2.14.​ Access to Services. Licensee will not deny service or access, or otherwise
           discriminate on the availability, rates, terms, or conditions of Broadband Internet
           Services provided to residential subscribers on the basis of race, color, creed,
           religion, ancestry, national origin, gender, sexual orientation, disability, age, familial
           status, marital status, or status with regard to public assistance. Licensee will comply
           with all applicable Federal, state, and local laws and regulations related to
           non-discrimination. Licensee will not deny or discriminate against any group of actual
           or potential residential subscribers in the City regarding access, rates, and terms and
           conditions of Broadband Internet Services on the basis of income level or other
           demographics of the local area in which the group may be located.

    2.15.​ Public Benefits. Licensee agrees to use good faith to consult with the City on
           developing initiatives designed to benefit the public, which may include increasing
           access to Broadband Internet Services, improving digital literacy, and bridging the
           digital divide.


3.​ City’s Obligations.

    3.1.​   Emergency Removal or Relocation by City. In the event of a public emergency that
            creates an imminent threat to the health, safety, or property of City or its residents,
            City may remove or relocate the applicable portions of the Network Facilities without
            prior notice to Licensee. City will, however, make best efforts to provide prior notice
            to Licensee before making an emergency removal or relocation. In any event, City
            will promptly provide to Licensee a written description of any emergency removals or
            relocations of Licensee’s Network Facilities. Licensee will reimburse City for its
            actual, reasonable, and documented costs or expenses incurred for any such work
            performed by City, the direct cause of which was Licensee’s construction, installation,
            operation, maintenance, repair, or removal of its Network Facilities. Licensee’s
            obligation to reimburse City under this section will be separate from Licensee’s
            obligation to pay the License Fee (as defined below).

    3.2.​   Abandoned Network Facilities. Network Facilities not used for more than one year
            shall be considered abandoned (“Abandoned Network Facilities”). Aboveground
            Abandoned Network Facilities shall be removed by Licensee at Licensee’s expense
            as soon as practicable. Underground Abandoned Network Facilities shall be
            removed at Licensee’s expense when commercially reasonable, or the City may
            permit Licensee to leave underground Abandoned Network Facilities in place, or the



GOOGLE FIBER CONFIDENTIAL
License Agreement Between City of Bluffdale, UT and Google Fiber Utah, LLC | Page 5 of 13
                                                                              Google Fiber Contract No. n157357



            City may agree to have underground Abandoned Network Facilities conveyed to the
            City. Abandoned Network Facilities does not include Network Facilities intended for
            emergency use, redundant Network Facilities, or Network Facilities intended to meet
            future demand or capacity.

    3.3.​   Relocation to Accommodate Governmental Purposes. If Licensee’s then-existing
            Network Facilities would interfere with City’s planned use of the Public ROW or other
            City property for a legitimate governmental purpose, such as the construction,
            installation, repair, maintenance, or operation of a new water, sewer, or storm drain
            line, or a public road, curb, gutter, sidewalk, park, or recreational facility, Licensee
            will, upon written notice from City, relocate its Network Facilities at Licensee’s own
            expense to such other location or locations in the Public ROW as may be mutually
            agreed by the parties, taking into account the needs of City’s governmental purpose
            and Licensee’s interest in maintaining the integrity and stability of its Network.
            Licensee will relocate its Network Facilities within a commercially reasonable period
            of time agreed to by the parties, taking into account the urgency of the need for
            relocation, the difficulty of the relocation, and other relevant facts and circumstances,
            except that City may not require Licensee to relocate or remove its Network Facilities
            with less than one hundred eighty (180) days’ notice.

    3.4.​   Relocation to Accommodate Non-Governmental Purposes.                    If Licensee’s
            then-existing Network Facilities would interfere with (a) City’s planned use of the
            Public ROW for a non-governmental (e.g., commercial) purpose, or (b) a third-party’s
            use of the Public ROW, Licensee will not be required to relocate its Network Facilities
            unless the City or the third party enters into an agreement with the Licensee under
            which City the third party would, at a minimum, (a) identify and provide a new
            location for Licensee’s Network Facilities that is acceptable to Licensee, (b) agree to
            a commercially reasonable period of time for the relocation, which in no event will be
            less than 180 days; and (c) agree to reimburse all of Licensee’s reasonable direct
            and indirect costs, expenses, and losses associated with the relocation.

    3.5.​   Non-Discrimination. City will at all times treat Licensee in a non-discriminatory
            manner as compared to other non-incumbent holders of local or state franchise
            authority offering facilities-based broadband Internet access services.


    3.6.​   Post-Removal Restoration of Public ROW. When removal or relocation is required
            under this Agreement, Licensee will, after the removal or relocation of the Network
            Facilities, at its own cost, repair and return the Public ROW in which the facilities
            were located to a safe and satisfactory condition in accordance with the
            construction-related conditions and specifications as established by City.

    3.7.​   Inspection by City. The Installation of the Network Facilities shall be subject to
            inspection and approval by the City as provided by City code.

                                        MEMORANDUM

To:   Bluffdale City Council
From: Fredric Donaldson, Bluffdale City Attorney
Date: December 5, 2024
Re:   Ordinance 2024-30 Amending definition of “disposition” of property


        The Utah State Code requires that a public hearing before the City Council be held
before a City disposes of a “significant parcel of real property,” and it allows cities to define
what that term means. Utah Code 10-8-2(4). The City has adopted a broad definition of a
“significant parcel of property” that states, “any parcel of City-owned property that has a
reasonable value in excess of ten thousand dollars ($10,000).” Bluffdale City Code 7.50.010.
        Similarly, the City has adopted a broad definition of “disposition.” According to
Bluffdale City Code, “disposition” means “any sale, exchange, lease, encumbrance or other
conveyance of property.” Id. This broad definition also includes conveyances of easements
for public-use to government entities and utility companies, meaning even a sewer
easement to Jordan Basin Improvement District within a City right-of-way or on other City
property would be required to have a public hearing before the City Council.
        It is recommended that the definition of “disposition” be amended to exclude
conveyances of non-perpetual easements, leases, and licenses for public use. This would
allow the City to streamline conveyances of public utility and other public easements,
leases, and licenses on its rights-of-way and other City-owned property. It is also
recommended that the definition of “property” be amended to clarify that it includes land
and improvements upon the land.
                                CITY OF BLUFFDALE, UTAH

                                 ORDINANCE NO. 2024-30

 AN ORDINANCE OF THE BLUFFDALE CITY COUNCIL AMENDING 7.50.010 TO INDICATE
 THAT A DISPOSITION OF SIGNIFICANT REAL PROPERTY EXCLUDES THE CONVEYANCE
    OF NON-PERPETUAL EASEMENT, LEASE, OR LICENSE FOR A PUBLIC PURPOSE

        WHEREAS, Chapter 7.50 of the Bluffdale City Code governs the disposition of
significant parcels of real property;

       WHEREAS, the City Council desires to amend the definition of disposition in
7.50.010 to exclude conveyances of non-perpetual easements, leases, or licenses for a
public purpose;

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT ORDAINED BY THE CITY COUNCIL OF THE CITY OF BLUFFDALE,
UTAH AS FOLLOWS:

       Section 1.     Adoption. Ordinance 2024-30 is hereby adopted, amending Chapter
7.50.010 of the Bluffdale City Code as shown in Exhibit A.

       Section 2.      Amendment of Conflicting Ordinances. If any ordinances,
resolutions, policies, or zoning maps of the City heretofore adopted are inconsistent
herewith they are hereby amended to comply with the provisions hereof. If they cannot be
amended to comply with the provisions hereof, they are hereby repealed.

        Section 3.    Severability. If any section, part, or provision of this Ordinance is
held invalid or unenforceable, such provision shall be deemed a separate, distinct, and
independent provision, and such invalidity or unenforceability shall not affect any other
portion of this Ordinance and all sections, parts, provisions and words of this Ordinance
shall be severable.

      Section 4.     Effective Date. This Ordinance shall become effective immediately
upon publication or posting as required by law.

7.50 DISPOSITION OF SIGNIFICANT REAL PROPERTY

7.50.010 Definitions

7.50.010 Definitions

DISPOSITION: Any sale, exchange, lease, encumbrance or other conveyance of property

              REQUEST FOR CITY COUNCIL ACTION

To:                                 Mayor and City Council
From:                               Caitlyn Tubbs, Planning Manager
Date:                               December 4, 2024
Business Date:                      December 11, 2024
Subject:                            Proposed amendments to the SD-X Gateway Zone to allow Satellite
                                    Communications Stations as a permitted use.
Applicant(s):                       inRange Solutions II LLC, represented by Casey Reno and Jon Fazio
Staff Presentation:                 Caitlyn Tubbs

SUMMARY:

The Applicant proposes the installation of a Satellite Communication Station at approximately 383 West 14600
South which is located in the SD-X Gateway Zone. The proposed installation would occupy approximately one-
half acre of space at the southern end of the parcel. The SD-X Gateway Zone currently does not allow Satellite
Communication Stations, so the Applicant has submitted a Text Amendment request to modify the code to
allow the stations as a permitted use.

BACKGROUND:

The SD-X Gateway Zone is located at the eastern gateway of Bluffdale at the intersection of 14600 South and
Pony Express Road. This Zone is governed by Section 11.110.110 in the Bluffdale City Code, which was enacted
on July 25, 2018 1 through Ordinance 2018-20. The SD-X Gateway Zone included a concept site plan with three
hotels. The SD-X Gateway Zone was amended by the City Council on October 9, 2019, through Ordinance 2019-
19 2 and entitled the site to the development of a full-service hotel and three residential buildings. The
residential buildings are entitled to up to 500 units with a minimum height of 10 stories and no maximum
building height.

The Applicant has proposed the development of a Satellite Communication Station on the southern end of the
parcel governed by the SD-X Gateway Zone. The Station would include a half-acre enclosure with 40
communications antennas. According to the Applicant’s plan specs, the antennas would be spherical in shape
and approximately 2 meters (6.5 feet) in diameter. The station would be enclosed on four sides by an 8-foot-tall
metal fence and accessed via an existing gravel road.




1 City Council Staff Report (p. 55), Minutes (p. 4)
2 City Council Staff Report (p. 49), Minutes (p. 5)
PLANNING COMMISSION CONSIDERATIONS:

The Planning Commission held a public hearing on October 2, 2024 3, and November 6, 2024 4, and unanimously
forwarded a positive recommendation of the proposed Text Amendments to the City Council, with an additional
recommendation that the Council consider the following:

    -   That the City obtain a third-party review of the Applicant’s Radiation Impact Analysis; and
    -   That project fencing and landscaping adhere to the requirements adopted in Bluffdale City Code
        11.160.160 Public Utility Substations; and
    -   That the City inquire with the City of West Jordan about their experience with permitting the same
        facilities and any impacts they have had on the community.

Following a public hearing on October 2, 2024, the Planning Commission requested the Applicant provide a
Radiation Impact Analysis (RIA) discussing anticipated radiation impacts on the surrounding area if the proposal
is approved. The Applicant provided the Analysis in advance of the November 6, 2024, Planning Commission
meeting and the report indicated no adverse impacts were anticipated as the site currently exists. However,
future high-rise development on the site could potentially be impacted by the installation’s Main Beam. The
Planning Commission asked that the City obtain a third-party peer review of the RIA. Staff has made efforts to
find a third part radiation engineer but has, so far, been unable to contact one.

Staff contacted Larry Gardner from the West Jordan City Planning Department, who indicated these satellite
communications stations are permitted in their city as public utilities substations and required site plan and
building permit approval prior to construction. Further, Mr. Gardner indicated the City and the public did not
have concerns regarding radiation or signal impacts following installation of the satellite communications
stations.

                                CITY OF BLUFFDALE, UTAH

                                 ORDINANCE NO. 2024-26

    AN ORDINANCE AMENDING CHAPTER 11.110 SD-X GATEWAY ZONE TO ALLOW
   SATELLITE COMMUNICATIONS STATIONS AS A PERMITTED USE AND ADOPTING A
             DEFINITION FOR SATELLITE COMMUNICATIONS STATION

       WHEREAS, Chapter 11.110 of the Bluffdale City Code governs the Special District
(SD-X) Gateway Zone and includes a concept plan and list of approved land uses for a 6.78-
acre parcel at 383 West 14600 South;

    WHEREAS, the Applicant proposes to construct a 20,578–square-foot Satellite
Communications Station at the southern end of the subject parcel.

        WHEREAS, the Applicant has proposed an amendment to the SD-X Gateway Zone to
allow Satellite Communications Stations as permitted uses and to enact a definition for
Satellite Communications Stations.

      WHEREAS, a notice of public hearing for the proposed amendments was provided
pursuant to the Bluffdale City Code and Utah State Code;

       WHEREAS, on November 6, 2024, the Planning Commission conducted a public
hearing and unanimously forwarded a positive recommendation of the proposed Text
Amendment to the City Council, with findings outlined in the Council staff report.

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT ORDAINED BY THE CITY COUNCIL OF THE CITY OF BLUFFDALE,
UTAH AS FOLLOWS:

       Section 1.     Adoption. Ordinance 2024-26 is hereby adopted, amending Chapter
11.110 of the Bluffdale City Code as shown in Exhibit A.

       Section 2.      Amendment of Conflicting Ordinances. If any ordinances,
resolutions, policies, or zoning maps of the City heretofore adopted are inconsistent
herewith they are hereby amended to comply with the provisions hereof. If they cannot be
amended to comply with the provisions hereof, they are hereby repealed.

        Section 3.    Severability. If any section, part, or provision of this Ordinance is
held invalid or unenforceable, such provision shall be deemed a separate, distinct, and
independent provision, and such invalidity or unenforceability shall not affect any other
portion of this Ordinance and all sections, parts, provisions and words of this Ordinance
shall be severable.
      Section 4.     Effective Date. This Ordinance shall become effective immediately
upon publication or posting as required by law.

PASSED AND APPROVED: December 11, 2024
