EXHIBIT A
11.20.020 General Deﬁnitions

…

Satellite Communications Station: A station and/or facility located on the Earth’s surface or
within the major portion of the Earth’s atmosphere intended for communication with one or
more stations of the same kind by means of one or more reﬂecting Satellites or other
extraterrestrial objects in space. A satellite communication station shall include any
exterior equipment two meters in diameter or in width or in length used to receive direct
broadcast satellite service, including but not limited to direct-to-home satellite service; to
receive or transmit ﬁxed wireless signals via satellite; to receive video-programming service
via multipoint distribution services; multichannel multipoint distributions services; to
receive or transmit ﬁxed wireless signals other than via satellite; or to receive broadcast
communication signals. Such term shall include any mount, pole or other elevating device
supporting the same.

…

11.11.110.010 Purpose And Zone Characteristics

The SD-X Gateway Zone is established to recognize the unique placement of hospitality,
multi-family dwellings, and retail facilities at the eastern gateway to City of Bluffdale and
accommodate development and construction requirements of these facilities on a
prominent but challenging site due to slopes, property conﬁguration, and easements and
rights-of-way of large distribution utilities. The zone allows for hospitality, multi-family
dwellings, and associated retail focused commercial uses.

    1. Permitted uses:

       Bar, or other hotel alcohol sub-license as set forth in Utah Code § 32B-8b-102.

       Hotel.

       Personal service establishment.

       Recreation and entertainment, indoor.

       Restaurant, general.

       Multi-family dwellings, up to ﬁve hundred (500) total units.
      Retail, general.

      Satellite Communications Station, up to one half-acre (21,780 square feet) in size.

11.11.110.020 Project Area And Concept Plan

   1. The project area includes the entire 6.78 acre parcel as described in Figure 1.

   2. Conceptual Site Plan: Figure 1 of this section is the SD-X Gateway conceptual site
      plan:
      FIGURE 1: Project Area And Conceptual Site Plan For Scope Of Development




11.11.110.030 Setback Requirements

The following minimum setback requirements shall apply in the SD-X Gateway Zone. Each
setback is measured from the property line of the lot or parcel.

   1. Building setbacks abutting a public street:

          1. Front Setback: Each lot or parcel abutting a public street shall have a
             minimum front setback of twenty feet (20').

          2. Side And Rear Setback: Each lot or parcel abutting a public street shall have
             a minimum side and rear setback of twenty feet (20'). A total reduction of the
             rear and side setbacks of up to twenty feet (20') may be approved if doing so
             does not violate the adopted Building and Fire Codes.

   2. Parking setbacks for lots or parcels abutting a public street:

          1. Along 14600 South: Parking abutting 14600 South Street shall have a
             landscaped setback of ten feet (10') from the edge of the public right-of-way.

          2. Along Pony Express Drive: Parking abutting Pony Express Drive shall have a
             setback of zero feet (0') from the public right-of-way. Owner shall install tire
              stoppers to ensure parked vehicles do not project over property lines, and
              provide landscaping, in accordance with the city's adopted landscaping
              requirements, in the area between edge of parking and the current street
              improvements.

          3. Side And Rear Setback: Along parcel lines not abutting a public street a
             landscaped setback of not less than ﬁve feet (5') and averaging not less than
             ten feet (10') shall be maintained.

11.11.110.040 Projections Into Setbacks

   1. Permitted: The following structures may be erected on or projected into any required
      setback:

          1. Fences and walls in conformance with applicable city ordinances and
             resolutions.

          2. Appropriate landscaping.

          3. Necessary appurtenances for utility service.

          4. Retaining walls, whether cast-in-place concrete, pre-cast concrete, or rock
             when required to accommodate grading of existing slopes for improvements.

   2. Setback Areas: The following structures may be erected on or projected into any
      required front or rear setback not more than four feet (4'), and into a side setback
      not more than two feet (2'):

          1. Cornices, eaves, sills, buttresses or other similar architectural features.

          2. Awnings, decks and planter boxes.

11.11.110.050 Building Height Requirements

Buildings shall have a minimum height of one (1) story with no limit on maximum stories.
The residential tower (as shown on Figure 1) shall have a minimum height of ten (10)
stories. Modiﬁcations to the minimum height of the residential tower will be allowed, within
reason, to accommodate site, design, or market conditions which prohibit feasibility of the
tower. Any modiﬁcation to the minimum height of the residential tower will be reviewed and
approved in accordance with the SD-X Gateway Zone provisions set forth for site plan
reviews and approvals. Minimum height for the residential tower shall not apply to
buildings which are not classiﬁed as residential tower. All buildings and structures must
adhere to adopted building and ﬁre codes.

11.11.110.060 Distance Between Buildings
The distance between any building or structure and any other building or structure shall
satisfy the Building and Fire Codes in place at the time of site plan approval.

11.11.110.070 Lot Coverage

The sum total of all buildings, structures and parking in the SD-X Gateway Zone shall not
exceed eighty ﬁve percent (85%) for the total area of the parcel or project.

11.11.110.080 Architectural Standards

   1. Architecture: The architecture shall be the primary tool to create a strong sense of
      identity and place throughout the special development zone. All buildings will be
      articulated with use of appropriate scale, form, materials, and colors carefully
      considered and incorporated throughout the development phases. However,
      different architectural styles will be considered and allowed in order to provide
      variety and identity to this area. Images of sample acceptable architectural styles
      are included in section 11-11K-1411.11.110.140.

   2. Materials: More than one material shall be required on all building facades.

          1. Primary Material: More than one primary material shall be required on all
             building facades. Building primary materials shall consist of metal,
             composite metal panel, stone, EFIS, concrete, GFRC, tile or other similar
             material as approved by Architectural Control Committee. Images of sample
             acceptable materials are included in section 11-11K-1411.11.110.140.

          2. Mechanical Equipment: All mechanical equipment shall be screened from
             view, either by enclosure, parapet wall or line of sight.

   3. Entry Features: Buildings shall have notable entry features which could include:

          1. An arch or canopy over entry doors.

          2. Colors or materials that highlight the entry.

          3. Entry doors and lobby that extend beyond or are recessed behind the main
             footprint of the building.

11.11.110.090 Parking, Loading And Access

   1. Requirements: Due to the nature of the several uses in a hospitality, retail, and
      multi-family mixed use development, such as residents, guests, restaurants, spas,
      retail, and convention meeting facilities, a shared parking and access plan will be
      developed and provided to the city for review as part of a complete site plan. The
      applicant shall submit a professionally prepared tabulation of shared parking
      calculations which demonstrates to the Land Use Authority that the parking
      demand of the assorted land uses will be met, based on the city’s adopted parking
      requirements in place at the time of site plan approval or based on site plan parking
      analysis recommendations approved by the Land Use Authority. The spaces shall be
      hard surfaced with asphalt or concrete and be accessed from a public road by a
      hard surfaced, composed of asphalt or concrete, drive approach which meets the
      requirements set forth in chapter 12 11.120 of this title.

   2. Supply Loading And Unloading Areas: Loading and unloading areas shall be located
      in an area that can be secured and visually obstructed from public access. Further,
      loading and unloading shall not occur on a public street.

11.11.110.100 Project Plan Approval

   1. Any request for project plan approval in the SD-X Gateway Zone is subject to any
      and all applicable city resolutions, standards, speciﬁcations, and ordinances,
      including, but not limited to, the subdivision ordinance, design guidelines
      requirements, and standard speciﬁcations.

   2. Site plans for projects in the SD-X Gateway Zone may be reviewed and approved
      administratively by staff in accordance with the provisions set forth in chapter
      1511.150 of this title.

11.11.110.110 Signage

   1. Signs: Signs are allowed in the SD-X Gateway Zone in accordance with the
      provisions in subsection B of this section. As this development is located in a highly
      visible area of the eastern gateway to the city, signage is a critical component to the
      success of a gateway development. The use of LED materials and electronic
      displays is permitted on all approved signs in accordance with the standards set
      forth in subsections 11-22-14G7 through 11-22-14G10 chapter 11.220 of this title.

   2. Types Of Signage Allowed:

          1. Highway Appurtenant Freestanding Sign: One (1) sign, not to exceed sixty
             feet (60') in height and a total signage area of six hundred (600) square feet,
             which shall have an architectural base consistent with the approved
             architectural requirements and shall have a design approval from the
             architectural review committee. Co-location of uses is encouraged on this
             sign.

          2. On-Site Appurtenant Freestanding Signs: One (1) appurtenant freestanding
             sign on Pony Express Drive will be allowed, each not exceeding thirty feet
              (30') in height and one hundred ﬁfty (150) square feet in area, which shall
              have an architectural base consistent with the approved architectural
              requirements and shall have a design approval from the architectural review
              committee.

          3. Monument Signs: One (1) monument sign on-site near the shared entrance
             on 14600 South and one (1) each monument sign at each project site
             entrance on Pony Express Drive, each of which shall have an architectural
             base consistent with the approved architectural requirements and shall have
             a design approval from the architectural review committee. Monument signs
             shall be no taller than nine feet (9') and have an area no greater than one
             hundred ﬁfty (150) square feet.

          4. Building Signage: Branding of hotels may be accomplished with signage
             complying with standards of branded hotels. Signage shall be equal to pan
             channel construction with backlighting which may have translucent faces.
             Multi-family and commercial facilities may also have building mounted
             signage, such as backlight pan channel signs which may have translucent
             faces. Size of building mounted signs shall be limited in area to two (2)
             square feet per linear foot of the face of the building on which it is installed.
             Two (2) signs per apartment building are allowed and one (1) sign per face of
             the hotel is allowed. The tower may have up to two (2) signs for the residential
             use and one (1) sign for each retail tenant. The allowable area for any face
             not used may be applied to the faces on which signs are installed.

          5. LED Building Array: In addition to branding signs, the primary hotel may
             include an LED building array on the eastern side of full service hotel which
             may wrap to the northern exposure of I-15 up to ﬁve thousand (5,000) square
             feet in size and shall comply with city ordinances as they may change from
             time to time. The array shall be equipped with light sensors and adjustable
             brightness.

          6. Safety signage as required by the Federal Communications Commission
             (FCC) for Satellite Communications Stations.

11.11.110.120 Other Requirements

The following requirements are in addition to the requirements found in this title or any
other applicable resolution or ordinance:

   1. Landscaping: Each lot or parcel shall be completely landscaped except those areas
      used for buildings or parking. A minimum of ﬁfteen percent (15%) of the total area of
   the lot or parcel shall be landscaped. Individual project phases located in the SD-X
   Gateway Zone may contain less than ﬁfteen percent (15%) landscaping so long as at
   least ﬁfteen percent (15%) of the total developed project area is landscaped.

       1. Landscaping for each building (or bonding to complete) shall be required
          prior to the issuance of a certiﬁcate of occupancy. In order to avoid damage
          or destruction to new landscaping, the required landscaping for each
          building will be deﬁned within the phasing plan for construction.

       2. For the purpose of calculating the requirement of ﬁfteen percent (15%)
          landscaping, outdoor activities spaces and improvements such as plazas,
          walks, pool decks, and other outdoor hard-surface improvements shall be
          considered part of the landscaping in minimal amounts if it is being used to
          beautify and ease transition into landscaping zones. Sidewalks and parking
          spaces shall not count toward minimum landscaping requirements.

2. In accordance with section 11-15-6 chapter 11.150 of this title, all landscaping shall
   comply with the landscaping requirements in sections 11-12-7 and 11-15-5 section
   11.120.070 and section 11.150.050. All required landscaping must be installed, or a
   landscaping bond must be posted (only if between the months of October and May),
   prior to the issuance of a certiﬁcate of occupancy.

3. Design Guidelines: Projects in the SD-X Gateway Zone will adhere to the design
   guidelines for commercial buildings as set forth in section 11-15-6 11.150.060 of
   this title.

4. Continuous Development: Improvements for the SD-X Gateway Zone shall be
   constructed as a continuous project from start to ﬁnish. Phasing for construction of
   buildings, facilities and other project elements will be allowed. For continuous
   development, construction of each building of an approved project site plan shall
   begin no more than twelve (12) months after occupancy permit is issued for a
   previously completed building of the approved site plan. The city shall not issue a
   certiﬁcate of occupancy for the residential tower prior to the issuance of a
   certiﬁcate of occupancy for the hotel. One (1) level of structured parking for the
   hotel shall be constructed at the same time as two (2) levels of structured parking
   for the apartment buildings. Modiﬁcations to construction phasing will be allowed,
   within reason, to accommodate site, design, or market conditions which prohibit
   these time parameters. Any modiﬁcations to the construction phasing will be
   reviewed and approved in accordance with the SD-X Gateway Zone provisions set
   forth for site plan reviews and approvals.
11.11.110.130 Architectural Review Committee

The construction of any building, sign, or other structure within the project, including the
design, architecture, exterior elevations, exterior ﬁnishes and other architectural attributes
will ﬁrst be approved by the architectural review committee. Conﬁrmation of the
architectural review committee's approval must be submitted along with all site and
building permit applications. The architectural review committee will have the right to
establish standardized construction guidelines for the project which are binding on the
owners. No building or structure within the project will be reconstructed, altered, added to
or maintained in such a fashion as to alter, in any material respect, the architectural
appearance, character, motif or functional purpose of such item, unless such alteration is
ﬁrst approved in writing by the architectural review committee. The architectural review
committee is not responsible for determining compliance with structural and Building
Codes, solar ordinances, Zoning Codes or other governmental restrictions, all of which are
the responsibility of the applicable owner. The architectural review committee shall consist
of as many persons, but not less than three (3), as the declarant, during the declarant
control period, or the owners, following the expiration of the declarant control period, may
from time to time appoint. At least one (1) member shall be a professional in the ﬁeld of
architecture.

                                                       STAFF REPORT
                                                      November 1, 2024

To:          Bluffdale Planning Commission
Prepared By: Caitlyn Tubbs, Planning Manager

Re:      Proposed Text Amendments to create a definition of “Satellite Communications Station”
         and allow Satellite Communications Stations as permitted uses in the SD-X Gateway Zone.

         Application No.: 2024-36
         Applicant:       inRange Solutions II LLC, represented by Casey Reno
         Request:         (I) Review the proposed Text Amendment to the SD-X Gateway Zone to
                          allow Satellite Communication Stations; (II) hold a public hearing; and (III)
                          forward a recommendation of the proposed amendments to the City
                          Council.

SUMMARY
The Applicant proposes the installation of a Satellite Communication Station at approximately 383
West 14600 South which is located in the SD-X Gateway Zone. The proposed installation would
occupy approximately one-half acre of space at the southern end of the parcel. The SD-X Gateway
Zone currently does not allow Satellite Communication Stations, so the Applicant has submitted a
Text Amendment request to modify the code to allow the stations as a permitted use. The Planning
Commission reviewed this application on October 2, 2024, and tabled the item after requesting
additional information.

BACKGROUND
The SD-X Gateway Zone is located at the eastern gateway of Bluffdale at the intersection of 14600
South and Pony Express Road. This Zone is governed by Section 11.110.110 in the Bluffdale City
Code, which was enacted on July 25, 2018 1 through Ordinance 2018-20. The SD-X Gateway Zone
included a concept site plan with three hotels. The SD-X Gateway Zone was amended by the City
Council on October 9, 2019, through Ordinance 2019-19 2 and entitled the site to the development of
a full-service hotel and three residential buildings. The residential buildings are entitled to up to 500
units with a minimum height of 10 stories and no maximum building height.


1 City Council Staff Report (p. 55), Minutes (p. 4)
2 City Council Staff Report (p. 49), Minutes (p. 5)
                                                                   Application 2024-36 SD-X Gateway Zone Text Amendments
                                                                             November 6, 2024 Planning Commission Meeting
                                                                                                                   Page 1
The Applicant has proposed the development of a Satellite Communication Station on the southern
end of the parcel governed by the SD-X Gateway Zone. The Station would include a half-acre
enclosure with 40 communications antennas. According to the Applicant’s plan specs, the antennas
would be spherical in shape and approximately 2 meters (6.5 feet) in diameter. The station would be
enclosed on four sides by an 8-foot-tall metal fence and accessed via an existing gravel road.




The Planning Commission requested additional information regarding the radiation and security
standards the site would be required to meet. The Applicant has submitted a Radiation Hazard
Analysis (Exhibit E) along with photographs of other existing sites (Exhibit F) for the Planning
Commission’s consideration.

ANALYSIS
During the October 2, 2024 meeting, the Planning Commission expressed concern regarding the
appearance and potential radiation impacts of the proposed facility. The Applicant has supplied
photographs of similar sites and the security fencing proposed to surround the subject enclosure
(Exhibit F).

The Applicant’s Radiation Hazard Analysis (Exhibit E) indicates the site will not exceed industry
standards for radiation in the surrounding area and as the site currently exists. Higher levels of
radiation are expected within the enclosure and the Analysis states the equipment can and will be
remotely powered down before maintenance crews access the enclosure where higher radiation
levels are anticipated.

There may be additional radiation concerns, however, as the site develops in the future. The
Analysis states, “RF levels may potentially exceed general population MPE limits in the main beam
out to a distance of up to 240.8 meters. (Pg. 2)” The Analysis continues, “…the antenna main beam
                                                        Application 2024-36 SD-X Gateway Zone Text Amendments
                                                                  November 6, 2024 Planning Commission Meeting
                                                                                                        Page 2
is not accessible to the general public because the antenna minimum elevation angle will be 25
degrees and no nearby buildings or elevated terrain will intersect with this portion of the antenna
main beam 3.” Since the site is currently vacant there are minimal buildings in the surrounding area
that may be affected by the antenna main beam radiation, however, if the existing entitled users
begin to construct they will likely be impacted by the antenna main beam and would interfere with
the signal in return.

Section 11.160.160 of the Bluffdale City Code regulates landscaping requirements for public utilities
substations. This includes a 10’ irrigated landscape setback with medium-sized trees and a CMU,
concrete panel, or brick enclosure wall. As proposed, the text amendment would not require any
perimeter landscaping and would allow an 8-foot-tall metal fence surrounding the enclosure. The
Planning Commission should determine whether Satellite Communications Stations should be
subject to the requirements in Section 11.160.160 or if the proposed fence will suffice.

CONSIDERATIONS FOR PLANNING COMMISSION
  1) The Planning Commission should consider whether the proposed text amendments are in
     keeping with the purpose of the SD-X Gateway Zone, which is: “to recognize the unique
     placement of hospitality, multi-family dwellings, and retail facilities at the eastern gateway
     to City of Bluffdale…”
  2) If the proposed Text Amendment is to be approved, the Planning Commission should
     consider whether design standards and/or additional setbacks or landscaping requirements
     should be applied to the proposed land use.

PUBLIC NOTICE
A notice of this public hearing was provided as follows:
   - Published on the Utah Public Notice Website on September 20, 2024.
   - Published on the City of Bluffdale’s Official Website on September 20, 2024
   - Posted at City Hall on September 20, 2024.

The Planning Commission opened and closed the public hearing for this item on Wednesday,
October 2, 2024 (Packet, Minutes). Since the public hearing was previously opened and closed, no
additional notice has been provided of the Planning Commission’s review. Once scheduled for a
hearing before the City Council, an additional notice will be sent in compliance with Bluffdale City
Code 11.30.060.

                                 Radiation Hazard Analysis
                                      Gateway Earth Station

Introduction

This analysis calculates the non-ionizing radiation levels for a SpaceX Services, Inc. (“SpaceX
Services”) gateway earth station. The calculations performed in this analysis comply with the
methods described in FCC Office of Engineering and Technology Bulletin, Number 65 (Edition
97-01) (“Bulletin 65”). This analysis demonstrates that SpaceX Services gateways are generally
compliant and will not result in exposure levels exceeding the applicable radiation hazard limits,
and any radiation hazard that may exist will be mitigated by limited access and various protocols
to ensure safe exposure levels.

Bulletin 65 and Section 1.1310 of the Commission's rules specify two separate tiers of exposure
limits: one for Occupational/Controlled Exposures and one for General Population/Uncontrolled
Exposures. Limits for Occupational/Controlled Exposures apply in situations where persons are
exposed as a consequence of their employment and are fully aware of and can control their
exposure. These limits also apply in situations where a person is transient through a location where
such limits would otherwise apply provided the person is made aware of the potential for exposure.
The limits for General Population/Uncontrolled Exposure apply in situations in which the general
public may be exposed, or in which persons that are exposed as a consequence of their employment
may not be fully aware of the potential for exposure or cannot exercise control over their exposure.
SpaceX Services will deploy its gateways in Occupational/Controlled Exposures Environments.
The Maximum Permissible Exposure (“MPE”) limits for the Ka-band and E-band frequencies used
by these gateways are a power density equal to 5 mW/cm2 averaged over a six-minute period (for
Occupational/Controlled Exposure) and 1 mW/cm2 averaged over a thirty-minute period (for
General Population/Uncontrolled Exposure).1

As described in the definitional section below, this report analyzes the maximum power density
levels in the vicinity of a gateway antenna in five regions: (1) the far field, (2) the near field, on-
axis, (3) the near field, off-axis, (4) near the main reflector surface, and (5) between the main
reflector and the feed mouth. These radiation regions were analyzed using the definitions and
formulas in Bulletin 65 for aperture antennas. The results of this analysis are summarized in Tables
1-3, which identifies the potential exposure under worst-case operating conditions.

Gateway Description

The gateway antenna is a 1.85-meter parabolic dish capable of steering its beams to track NGSO
satellites passing within its field of view. At the antenna flange, the maximum transmit power is
40W for Ka-band and 40W for E-band. Note the antenna is capable of transmitting simultaneously
in both bands. Although unlikely in practice, for purposes of this analysis we have conservatively
assumed that the gateway can transmit at 100% duty cycle.

1
    See 47 C.F.R. § 1.1310(e).

                                                  1
Explanation of the Analysis

The “Calculated Values” in Table 1 are the exposure rates for Ka-band calculated using the
formulae from Bulletin 65 for a system with continuous (100% transmit duty cycle) transmission.

The “Calculated Values” in Table 2 are the exposure rates for E-band calculated using the formulae
from Bulletin 65 for a system with continuous (100% transmit duty cycle) transmission.

The “Calculated Values” in Table 3 are the exposure rates when transmitting simultaneously in
Ka-band and E-band calculated using the formulae from Bulletin 65 for a system with continuous
(100% transmit duty cycle) transmission.

Results of Analysis

This analysis demonstrates that the SpaceX Services gateway is not a radiation hazard because it
does not exceed the MPE limits in generally accessible areas. These gateways will be located in
an area clearly marked with Radiation Hazard signage with no access by the general public. The
near field region creates no concern for the general public as it lies behind signage where only
authorized personnel may enter. RF levels may potentially exceed general population MPE limits
in the main beam out to a distance of up to 240.8 meters for Ka-band, 625.7 meters for E-band,
and 670.4 meters for Ka-band and E-band in the aggregate. However, outside of SpaceX’s
controlled facility, the antenna main beam is not accessible to the general public because the
antenna minimum elevation angle will be 25 degrees and no nearby buildings or elevated terrain
will intersect with this portion of the antenna main beam. Accordingly, there is no risk of radiation
exposure beyond the acceptable limits in generally accessible areas. Around the feed mouth (this
measurement is taken at a point between the feed and the sub-reflector), at the main reflector
surface, and in the near field on-axis the relevant radiation levels are exceeded. Power to the
transmitters will be turned off remotely whenever work needs to be performed in any of these
regions. Signage will mark the area for Radiation Hazard and access by qualified personnel only,
ensuring awareness and enhancing safety. Consequently, there is no risk of radiation exposure
beyond the acceptable limits.


Conclusion

This radiation hazard analysis demonstrates that SpaceX Services gateways will not result in
unacceptable radiation exposure levels.




                                                 2
Definitions

1) Far Field Region
                                                                                         0.6𝐷 2
The far field region extends outward from the antenna surface, beginning at a distance     𝜆
meters where the D is the diameter of the antenna.

2) Near Field Region:
The near field region is a volume co-incident with the direction of the main beam extending
                                                             𝐷2
outward from the antenna surface the length of the near field 4𝜆 meters.

3) Transition Region
The transition region is located between the near field region and the far field region. This
region has a power density that decreases with increasing distance. Therefore, the power density
in the transition region will be less than the maximum power density in the near field and more
than the maximum power density in the far field for the purpose of evaluating potential exposure.

4) Region Near the Antenna Surface
The power density near the antenna surface can be estimated as equal to four times the power
divided by the area of the main reflector surface (main reflector illumination is uniform).

5) Region between the Main Reflector and the Feed
The power radiated from the feed toward the reflector is conical in shape with the vertex at the
feed. The maximum power is at the feed mouth and can be estimated as four times the transmit
power divided by the area of the feed mouth.

RE: StarLink Satellite Communications Stations

From Larry Gardner <larry.gardner@westjordan.utah.gov>

     Caution: This email originated from outside of the organization. Do not click links or open
     attachments unless you recognize the sender and know the content is safe.
Caitlyn, answering your questions in Red:

1.        Did West Jordan require any ordinance amendments to allow these facilities or were they
already permitted by right? The use is a permitted use in the zones where they are located.
2.        What was the approval process for these installations? Site plan approval and building permit.
3.        Did West Jordan (either the internal staff or residents) have any concerns regarding radiation
exposure or signal interference with these facilities? No. We were assured that there would not be any
interference with existing facilities (one group is next to the airport and they did not have any issues with
it). The minimum safe distance for the public is 13 inches. The antennas are between 25 and 100 feet
from a road and over that distance from any office buildings. The only interference could be with
astronomy radio antennas/telescopes which we have none of in West Jordan.

To:                             Mayor and City Council

Business Date:                  December 11, 2024
Subject:                        Text Amendment enacting Section 11.160.310 of the Bluffdale City Code to
                                permit “Allowable Features” within residential rear setbacks pursuant to Utah
                                Code § 10-9a-540.
Applicant(s):                   City of Bluffdale
Staff Presentation:             Caitlyn Tubbs

RECOMMENDATION: To approve Ordinance 2024-27 for text amendments as recommended by the Planning
Commission on November 6, 2024.

BACKGROUND: The 2024 Utah State Legislature adopted House Bill 476 which enacted Utah Code §10-9a-540.
This Section identifies “Allowable Feature[s]” that must be allowed within rear setbacks in residential zones.
These Features include the following:

    -   A landing or walkout porch that:
            o Is no more than 32 square feet in size; and
            o Is used for ingress to and egress from the rear of the residential dwelling; or
    -   Window wells

Staff has drafted the attached redlines which allow these Features to project into setbacks for qualifying
detached residences.

FINDINGS FOR APPROVAL: Zoning text amendments are a legislative decision of the City Council, after
receiving a recommendation from the Planning Commission. Pursuant to Utah State Code, counties and
municipalities are obligated to observe Statutory requirements. The Planning Commission held a public hearing
on November 6, 2024, and unanimously forwarded a positive recommendation to the City Council.

SUPPORTING DOCUMENTATION:
Ordinance 2024-27
Planning Commission Staff Report dated November 1, 2024
                                CITY OF BLUFFDALE, UTAH

                                 ORDINANCE NO. 2024-27

AN ORDINANCE ENACTING SECTION 11.160.310 OF THE BLUFFDALE CITY CODE TO
PERMIT “ALLOWABLE FEATURES” WITHIN RESIDENTIAL REAR YARD SETBACKS TO
COMPLY WITH REQUIREMENTS IN UTAH STATE CODE ENACTED BY THE 2024
LEGISLATURE THROUGH HOUSE BILL 476.

WHEREAS the 2024 Utah State Legislature passed House Bill 476 which modified
subdivision review procedures and allowed specific features within required residential
rear setbacks;

WHEREAS the City proposes an amendment to Chapter 11.160 Supplementary Provisions,
enacting Section 11.160.310 to comply with the changes to Utah State Code permitting
“Allowable Features” within residential rear setbacks;

WHEREAS a notice of public hearing for the proposed amendments was provided pursuant
to the Bluffdale City Code and Utah State Code;

WHEREAS on November 6, 2024, the Planning Commission conducted a public hearing and
unanimously forwarded a positive recommendation of the proposed Text Amendment to
the City Council.

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT ORDAINED BY THE CITY COUNCIL OF THE CITY OF BLUFFDALE,
UTAH AS FOLLOWS:

       Section 1.     Adoption. Ordinance 2024-27 is hereby adopted, enacting Section
11.160.310 of the Bluffdale City Code as shown in Exhibit A.

       Section 2.      Amendment of Conflicting Ordinances. If any ordinances,
resolutions, policies, or zoning maps of the City heretofore adopted are inconsistent
herewith they are hereby amended to comply with the provisions hereof. If they cannot be
amended to comply with the provisions hereof, they are hereby repealed.

        Section 3.    Severability. If any section, part, or provision of this Ordinance is
held invalid or unenforceable, such provision shall be deemed a separate, distinct, and
independent provision, and such invalidity or unenforceability shall not affect any other
portion of this Ordinance and all sections, parts, provisions and words of this Ordinance
shall be severable.
      Section 4.     Effective Date. This Ordinance shall become effective immediately
upon publication or posting as required by law.

EXHIBIT A
11.160.310      Projections into Rear Setbacks for Detached Residences
A. For detached residences the following items shall be permitted to encroach into the
required rear setback:
        1. An uncovered landing or uncovered walkout porch up to thirty-two (32) square feet
in area and attached to the residential structure; and
        2. One or more window wells.
B. This provision shall not negate other allowed encroachments into setbacks as permitted
elsewhere in this Title.

Section identifies “Allowable Feature[s]” that are to be allowed within rear setbacks in residential
zones. These Features include the following:

      -   Window wells

qualifying detached residences.

MEMORANDUM
TO:            Mayor and City Council
FROM:          Ellen Oakman
DATE:          3 December 2024
SUBJECT:       Surplus Property Disposition – 2495 W 14200 S

The property being proposed for surplus disposition is part of one of the properties in the Santa
Rosa Subdivision. The most recent amended subdivision will be recorded soon, following this
disposition. There are two parcels to declare surplus and exchange properties – Parcel A and
Parcel B, located at the southern part of the property at 2495 W 14200 S. Parcel A is to be deeded
from the City to the property owner. Parcel B is to be deeded from the property owner to the
City. Each of the parcels is .10 acres. No appraisal is needed due to the small acreage as well as
the trade occurring. The Santa Rosa Plat Amendment will reflect the property exchange. The
subject property is zoned R-1-43 and CI.




                                                1
                                       RESOLUTION NO. 2024-80

A RESOLUTION OF THE CITY COUNCIL OF THE CITY OF BLUFFDALE, UTAH, DECLARING A SURPLUS
AND EXCHANGE OF PROPERTIES, LOCATED AT APPROXIMATELY 2495 W 14200 S, APPROXIMATELY
.10 ACRES FOR EACH PROPERTY OR .20 ACRES FOR BOTH, AND AUTHORIZING THE CITY MANAGER
TO DISPOSE OF THE PROPERTY PURSUANT TO BLUFFDALE CITY CODE SECTION §7.50

        WHEREAS, the City of Bluffdale is a municipal corporation and political subdivision of the
State of Utah (the “City) and owns a parcel of real property adjacent to the Santa Rosa Subdivision
located at approximately 2495 W 14200 S; and

        WHEREAS, the subject property is zoned Residential R-1-43 and Civil Institutional; and

        WHEREAS, this surplus disposition resolution will allow the City to surplus the property
(Parcel A described in exhibit A) and allow it to be potentially incorporated into the adjacent Santa Rosa
Plat; and

       WHEREAS, the City intends to exchange the surplus property (Parcel A) for other property
adjacent to City property (Parcel B) as described in exhibit A, hereto; and

        WHEREAS, the Bluffdale City Council (the “City Council”) has noticed and held a public hearing
as required and determined that it is in the best interest of the public health, safety, and welfare of City
to approve this surplus property disposition declaration to provide for the orderly development of the
Property and to facilitate economic development.

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE CITY COUNCIL OF THE CITY OF BLUFFDALE, UTAH:

        SECTION 1. Surplus Declaration. The City Council hereby declares the significant real
property shown in Exhibit A and listed as parcel #33043260390000 by the Salt Lake County Recorder’s
office as surplus and authorizes the City Manager to dispose of the property pursuant to the
requirements in Bluffdale City Code section §7.50.

        SECTION 2. Required Findings. In support of the surplus declaration and property exchange,
the following findings are made: The property being given to the Santa Rosa Plat is not necessary for
any public purpose and the property given to the City is useful for public purpose.

        SECTION 3. Severability. If any section, clause or portion of this Resolution is declared
invalid by a court of competent jurisdiction, the remainder shall not be affected thereby and shall
remain in full force and effect.

        SECTION 4. Effective Date. This Resolution shall become effective immediately upon
passage.

Subject:                       Text Amendment enabling Section 11.220.070 of the Bluffdale City Code to
                               permit restaurant drive-through facilities to have electronic menu boards
Applicant(s):                  City of Bluffdale
Staff Presentation:            Ellen Oakman

RECOMMENDATION: To approve Ordinance 2024-28 for text amendments as recommended by the Planning
Commission on November 6, 2024.

BACKGROUND: With the increase in new commercial businesses in the community, the City of Bluffdale has
initiated a text amendment to amend the sign requirements for restaurants with drive-throughs. Drive-through
restaurants commonly use electronic menu boards in other surrounding communities to have the most
effective drive-through experience. This text amendment to the permanent commercial sign code will
accommodate the use of electronic menu boards.

Staff has drafted the attached redlines which allow the use of electronic menu boards for restaurant drive-
through signs.

FINDINGS FOR APPROVAL: Zoning text amendments are a legislative decision of the City Council, after
receiving a recommendation from the Planning Commission. Pursuant to Utah State Code, counties and
municipalities are obligated to observe Statutory requirements. The Planning Commission held a public hearing
on November 6, 2024, and unanimously forwarded a positive recommendation to the City Council.

SUPPORTING DOCUMENTATION:
Ordinance 2024-28
Planning Commission Staff Report dated November 1, 2024

FACILITIES

       WHEREAS, the City proposes an amendment to Chapter 11.220 Permitted On-

                                       EXHIBIT A

Permitted On-Premises Permanent Signs
11.220.070.A.7

7. Restaurant Uses: Restaurants with drive-through facilities shall be allowed one on site
freestanding sign per drive-through lane, in addition to that otherwise permitted by this
section, according to the following requirements:
        a. The additional freestanding sign shall be located more than thirty feet (30') from
           the closest public right of way property line.
        b. The additional freestanding sign shall not be located more than five feet (5') from
           the drive-through lane.
        c.
        d. The maximum area of such sign shall be forty-five (45) square feet.
        e. Such sign shall not be taller than seven feet (7').
        f. Such signs may be illuminated by internal means only. internally, backlit, or
           illuminated digitally by means of EMC or LCD subject to requirements in section
           11.110.120.050 (A)(6).
        g. Such signs shall be identified and accommodated in the approved sign allowance
           and shall require a sign permit prior to installation.

To:          City of Bluffdale Planning Commission
Prepared By: Ellen Oakman, Associate Planner

Re:     City Initiated Text Amendment to Allow Restaurant Drive-Throughs to have Electronic Menu
        Boards
        Application No:         2024-35
        Applicant(s):           City of Bluffdale
        Request:                To consider forwarding a positive recommendation of proposed text
                                amendments to the Bluffdale City Land Use Regulations to allow
                                restaurant drive-through facilities to have electronic menu boards

SUMMARY
The City of Bluffdale has been welcoming many new businesses to the community, including new
restaurants with drive-through facilities. Drive-through restaurants commonly use electronic menu
boards in other surrounding communities to have the most effective drive-through experience. The
City of Bluffdale has initiated a text amendment to amend the permanent commercial sign
requirements for restaurants with drive-throughs to accommodate the use of these electronic
menu/message boards. The addition of electronic menu boards will not be an option for any other
commercial use except for restaurant drive-through facilities.

Staff has drafted the attached draft ordinance redlines which allow for the addition of electronic
menu boards in drive-through facilities.

7. Restaurant Uses: Restaurants with drive-through facilities shall be allowed one on site

       a. The additional freestanding sign shall be located more than thirty feet (30') from the
          closest public right of way property line.
